### Friendships Are What Will Save Journalism
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-20 12:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/the-continent-south-africa-kenya-nairobi-journalism-africa-industry-friendships-collaboration-magazines-sipho-kings-christine-mungai/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The opening sentence of my first message to Christine Mungai ended with the words: “fellow continent person.” It was August 2017, and we were the two Africans in that year’s cohort of Nieman journalism fellows at Harvard University. Although our home countries were about 4,000 kilometers (approximately 2,500 miles) apart in Africa, being from here means sharing a shorthand understanding of the world with your fellow Africans. That shorthand doesn’t have many structures to support it, however. Nine years after our first meeting, Christine and I are now working together to build one such structure: The Continent, a 30-page digital newspaper that aims to create a shared reality for Africans to learn about their communities, one another, and our continent. In a world that seeks to segregate us, this pan-African platform lets people literally read from the same page. As one reader said, the weekly provides “biting criticisms of power in contemporary Africa, with love as the engine.

### Focusing on Fans Captures the Magic of the World Cup
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-10 16:42 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/world-cup-fans-yutao-chen-film-photography-2026-new-york-new-jersey-spain-haiti-argentina-england/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

Photographer Yutao Chen first developed an interest in soccer during his childhood. As a lifelong follower of the sport, he seized the opportunity to cover the 2026 FIFA World Cup, traveling across the United States to document fan reactions throughout the tournament. He undertook the project independently, financing it himself rather than on assignment. Chen chose to work in film over digital, he explained, because he wanted these images to be imperfect, personal artifacts, shaped by his own perception rather than an exact record of events. This conversation has been edited and condensed for clarity. The first World Cup I remember watching was in 2006, when it was held in Germany. At first, the sport was an escape from reality for me, an experience to share with other fellow fans, forget our issues, and fully immerse ourselves in a game for 90 minutes. Later I realized soccer is one of the few ways I can connect with the world. I grew up in China, and my need to consume soccer news and analysis is what drove me into journalism.

### Student Uprisings in India Short-Circuit an Outdated Media Machine
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-05 15:25 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/student-uprisings-gen-z-india-protests-government-lapdog-media-journalism-modi-instagram-education-resign-exam-leak/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

If someone had told me that the vast, well-funded propaganda machine of India’s Bharatiya Janata Party (BJP) would finally be rendered powerless by a bunch of kids, I’d have laughed at the absurdity of it. Yet, here I am, witnessing a historic reckoning for the Indian government — and for Indian journalism — unfold before my eyes on Instagram Reels. While youth-led protests are not new to India — and have erupted across Southeast Asia in recent years — the current student mobilizations in the country are the most significant since Prime Minister Narendra Modi came to power in 2014. What began as a specific grievance stemming from a scandal that students uncovered in India’s educational testing system has quickly evolved into a broader critique of the government, and a powerful assertion of student agency. The sudden success of the movement has also revealed the changing nature of the mainstream media’s role in Indian society, and how ill-equipped it has been to cover these seismic societal shifts.

### When My Family Became My Newsroom
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-07-28 14:46 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/journalists-gaza-war-conflict-reporting-security-safety-air-strikes-internet-outages/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

When the war began, I was in Cairo. Like thousands of other Palestinians from Gaza who happened to be outside the strip, I suddenly found myself unable to return home. Overnight, I went from reporting on Gaza as someone who knew its streets, rhythms, and people intimately to relying almost entirely on mediated forms of access: news reports, social media, and daily phone calls with my family. Those calls were never just conversations. They always began with the questions any daughter would ask: Are you safe? Have you eaten? Where are you now? But they often continued with the questions of a reporter. Which neighborhoods had been hit? Were people able to communicate? Had anyone managed to reach the hospital? What were people saying that hadn't yet appeared in the news? While reporting on the war in Gaza, Palestinian journalist Athar Abu Samra worked from Cairo before returning home. Courtesy of Athar Abu Samra Over time, I realized that my family had become more than the people I worried about most.

Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-07-13 16:17 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/scene-in-boston-theater-podcasting-career-pivot-crime-journalism-audio-radio-hosting-student-lab-local-news/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

In the fall of 2012, just weeks into my Nieman Fellowship, our class went to see “Marie Antoinette” at the American Repertory Theater at Harvard University. I struggled with the play, and I remember feeling like I couldn’t grasp what was happening on stage, that I was missing something everyone else understood. When our curator, Ann Marie Lipinski, asked me later what I thought, I admitted as much. “Well,” she replied, turning down her mouth, “ you’re hard to please.” She wasn’t wrong. At the time, I was a crime reporter. I had recently founded Homicide Watch D.C., an independent digital publication dedicated to telling the stories of people whose lives might otherwise be forgotten. My work revolved around police, prosecutors, defense attorneys, and public records. Theater wasn't just outside my beat — it barely registered at all. If you had told me that evening at the start of my fellowship year that, more than a decade later, I would create a podcast (a podcast!) devoted to Boston theater (theater!), I would have laughed. I knew nothing about theater. Or podcasts.

### Shut Out: For the First Time, Iranian Journalists Are Denied Entry to Cover a FIFA World Cup
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-07-07 16:42 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/iranian-journalists-world-cup-banned-visas-sports-fifa-iran-trump-immigration-ban/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

At the age of 26, Iranian photojournalist Sajad Imanian has already covered several international sports events such as the Olympic Games and past World Cup soccer tournaments. He hardly expected his nationality would haunt him six years after leaving Iran to live in Australia, where he is working as a social media specialist with the Asian Football Confederation. An independent journalist, Imanian applied for a U.S. visa to cover the 2026 FIFA World Cup, co-hosted by the United States, Canada, and Mexico. When his appointment came up at the U.S. Consulate General in Sydney, he found himself being questioned over his Iranian military conscription status and possible trips to Lebanon and Gaza in an interview that took almost an hour. “I said I hadn’t even set foot in those places,” Imanian said. The Sydney-based photojournalist’s visa application to cover the games for the AFC was rejected because of his national origin, and his story is not an isolated case.

### 澳枪击事件嫌疑人为父子：曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”，行凶枪支为合法持有
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-16 02:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178049
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

Click to expand Image A person's mouth covered with tape bearing a message “no to executions” in Iran, in Paris in France, January 31, 2026. © 2026 Daniel Perron/Hans Lucas/AFP via Getty Images Within a span of only five months, between March 18 and the end of August 2026, Iran’s authorities announced the executions of 29 people in connection with recent protests. These executions were unlawful and in violation of international human rights law. Several protesters executed were teenagers aged 18 and 19. Scores more remain at imminent risk of execution. Some executions have been carried out in public to further instill fear, marking a significant escalation. Executions have followed grossly unfair and summary proceedings, ranging in protest-related cases from just over five weeks to less than eight months from arrest to execution. In most cases, no allegations of killings have been made and protesters have been convicted of vague national security charges and executed for acts such as allegedly damaging public property, throwing stones, or breaking into government buildings.

### Pakistan Moves to Expel Afghan Medical Students
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-09-10 02:17 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/09/09/pakistan-moves-to-expel-afghan-medical-students
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image King Edward Medical University, Lahore, Pakistan. © 2014 Razaawais141/Wikimedia Without warning, Pakistan has directed all medical and dental institutions to expel Afghan students and arrange to send them back to Afghanistan. It is the latest move by Pakistani authorities to summarily deport Afghans from the country. Especially for Afghan women students, being returned to Afghanistan ends their chances of ever becoming doctors or dentists, as the Taliban have banned all women from universities and medical training. Pakistan’s medical institutions have also suspended new admissions of Afghan nationals. Lahore’s King Edward Medical University directed 20 Afghan medical students, including 4 women and 7 students in their final year, to report within 24 hours to complete departure formalities. The nongovernmental Human Rights Commission of Pakistan estimates about 100 Afghan medical and dental students in Lahore may be affected, including 20 to 40 women.

### Australia: 25 Years of Abusive Offshore Detention
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-09-10 01:30 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/09/10/australia-25-years-of-abusive-offshore-detention-0
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Naoero, formerly Nauru, island, March 2023. © 2023 mtcurado/Getty Images (Sydney) – The Australian government has caused thousands of people seeking asylum to suffer grave abuses under its offshore detention system, which began in September 2001, Human Rights Watch said today. Over the past 25 years, Australia has forcibly transferred more than 5,000 asylum seekers to Naoero (formerly called Nauru) and Manus Island, Papua New Guinea (PNG). Those transferred have experienced inhumane conditions and serious harm to their mental and physical health. The United Nations refugee agency, other countries, and human rights groups including Human Rights Watch, have repeatedly pressed Australia to end offshore processing. “Australia’s offshore detention policy has caused profound harm to men, women, and children seeking refuge since its introduction 25 years ago,” said Annabel Hennessy, Australia researcher at Human Rights Watch.

### Indonesian Military Court Reduces Acid Attack Sentences
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-09-09 21:41 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/09/09/indonesian-military-court-reduces-acid-attack-sentences
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Andrie Yunus, deputy coordinator at KontraS, attending a protest in Bandar Lampung, Sumatra on February 5, 2026. © 2026 Project Multatuli/Adrian Mulya A military court in Jakarta reduced the prison sentences and overturned the dishonorable discharge of two of the four military personnel convicted for the March 2026 acid attack against human rights activist Andrie Yunus. The appellate decision, dated August 20, reduced the sentences of 2nd Sgt. Edi Sudarko and 1st Lt. Budhi Hariyanto Widhi Cahyono of the Indonesian military’s Strategic Intelligence Agency (BAIS) by six months, bringing both sentences to less than three years. The appeals court upheld the shorter sentences of the other two defendants, Capt. Nandala Dwi Prasetya and 1st Lt. Sami Lakka. On March 12, Yunus, 27, the deputy coordinator of KontraS, the nongovernmental Commission for the Disappeared and Victims of Violence, had just finished recording a podcast interview at the Indonesian Legal Aid Foundation when he was attacked with acid.

Her offense? Representing members of China’s Zion Church, a prominent underground Protestant congregation. While founding pastor Ezra Jin was freed from custody in July following high-level US intervention, eight other church members have remained wrongfully detained since their October 2025 arrests. Chinese leader Xi Jinping launched his “Sinicization of religion” policy in 2016, which included government regulations tightening controls over religious life. Beijing is not only punishing people for independent religious practice but also those defending their rights. Recently, authorities have continued to tighten surveillance over religious activities.

### A Growing Global Push for Free Education for Every Child
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-09-09 13:54 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/09/09/a-growing-global-push-for-free-education-for-every-child
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Students in a pre-primary school classroom in Tashkent, Uzbekistan, 2021. © 2021 Bede Sheppard / Human Rights Watch The global push to make free education accessible for every child gained momentum, as 81 states met at the United Nations in Geneva on August 31 to September 2 to discuss a proposed treaty that could strengthen children’s right to education and remove financial barriers that keep many out of school. The new optional protocol to the Convention on the Rights of the Child would recognize early childhood care and education as part of the right to education, guarantee at least one year of free public pre-primary education, and strengthen the promise of free public secondary education. The initiative has been led by Sierra Leone, the Dominican Republic, and Bulgaria, which already provide free education for their children from pre-primary through secondary. During the three-day meeting, Guatemala became the 62nd country to publicly support the initiative.

### Risk Perceptions of E-Cigarettes and the Evaluation of EVALI Messaging -- by Lawrence Jin, Donald S. Kenkel, Alan D. Mathios, Grace N. Phillips, Revathy Suryanarayana, Hua Wang
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35724#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

With the growth of sales of e-cigarettes and other alternative nicotine products, governments face the new challenge of crafting public health messages that provide information to consumers about these less risky but not risk-free products. Bayesian learning serves as the theoretical framework for our analysis of how smokers form their perceptions of the risks of e-cigarettes based on the risks of smoking. This paper consists of two parts. First, we conduct a cross-country descriptive analysis of government messaging and smokers’ perceptions of e-cigarette risks. We study a diverse group of eight countries: Australia, China, Indonesia, Japan, Malaysia, Sweden, the U.K. and the U.S. We find that public health messages range widely across the countries. In online cross-country surveys of adult smokers, on average perceptions of e-cigarette risks are associated with their perceptions of the risk of smoking and with their countries’ messaging.

### An Empirically-Grounded Theory of Voting Based on Prosocial Motives -- by Dan M. Bernhardt, Laurent Bouton, Stefan Krasa, Francesco Squintani
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35723#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We propose a model of costly voting in a canonical spatial setting that reconciles key empirical features of electoral turnout. We integrate citizens with prosocial voting motives who realize higher payoffs from voting when (i) they expect more fellow supporters to vote, (ii) such votes collectively matter more for winning probabilities, and (iii) they care more about which candidate wins. We establish existence of Nash equilibria satisfying an additional condition that ensures equilibria correspond to those in a finite setting with many voters. We derive how electoral primitives—e.g., voter ideology, voting costs, elite polarization, expected closeness—affect outcomes such as turnout, turnout rates, and winning margins. We relate the predictions to empirical findings both from existing studies and our original data work. With all three prosocial motive components, our model reconciles the empirical regularities qualitatively and quantitatively; while existing models fail on multiple dimensions.

### The Multigenerational Effects of Legal Access to the Pill on Infant Health -- by Hamid Noghanibehambari, David Slusky, Erdal Tekin, Hoa Vu
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35722#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We examine whether expanded legal access to the contraceptive pill for minors generated health benefits for subsequent generations. We use data from the restricted-use Natality files from 1970-2020, linking mothers to the level of access to the pill in their state and year of birth and the extent to which their mothers could access the pill as minors. Our results show that children whose mothers were born after expanded access to the pill as minors had better birth outcomes, with gains concentrated among Black families. Among affected births, legalization increased birth weight by 12-16 grams and reduced incidence of low birth weight by 2.7-3.4 percent. These magnitudes have economic significance, corresponding to approximately $113.8–$115.3 million in medical cost savings, measured in 2025 dollars, for the 2000 Black birth cohort alone.

### Politics-driven Market Access and Its Cost: Evidence from China’s Grand Canal -- by Ying Bai, Xiaoyu Bian, Ruixue Jia
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35721#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Transportation networks often radiate from national capitals, reflecting political priorities as much as economic logic. How costly is infrastructure built for politics rather than markets? We study China’s Grand Canal: when the Mongols moved the capital from the central plain to Beijing, the canal followed, bypassing the economic core it once served. Using a grid-year panel spanning five dynasties (8th–19th centuries), we show the route best suited to reach the capital predicts the canal’s actual path. This redesign reshaped market access and, through it, population. Counterfactual analysis shows the canal raised population by 2.5–6.1%, but its politically motivated redesign cost 1.1–2.2%.

### Does AI Assistance Enhance or Erode Expertise? Evidence from a Three-Month Field Experiment in Patent Drafting -- by David Autor, Tanya Rodchenko, Josh Martin, Zanna Iscenko, Scott Strand, David Pearl, Melissa Ferere
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35720#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Whether AI assistance builds or erodes professional expertise is unsettled. In a pre-registered three-month randomized controlled trial, we gave 133 practicing patent lawyers at eleven U.S. intellectual property law firms access to a custom AI drafting assistant and measured both their performance while using AI and their professional judgment afterward without it. All work was scored by blinded expert patent attorneys. Paralleling findings from other white-collar domains, AI access raised the quality of work delivered on benchmark patent drafting tasks at 10 days (0.34 SD, p = 0.03) and 90 days (0.38 SD, p = 0.01), with larger gains among junior lawyers. After three months, all subjects redlined an existing patent application without AI, a core task of patent practice requiring expert judgment. Treated lawyers outperformed controls by 0.32 SD (p = 0.04), but this advantage was concentrated entirely among senior lawyers (0.45 SD, p = 0.02).

### The Utility of AI Tools in Auditing Adherence to Pre-Analysis Plans -- by Jeffrey Clemens, Anwita Mahajan
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-09-10 13:59 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35719#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pre-analysis plans (PAPs) can improve research reproducibility by reducing researchers’ degrees of freedom, but their value depends on adherence. We argue that large language models (LLMs) can provide an efficient, scalable, and systematic way for authors and reviewers to assess adherence to PAPs. In an application to our own research, an LLM systematically identifies precommitted design choices, evaluates deviations, and diagnoses gaps in pre-specification, substantially reducing the human labor required for these tasks. However, variability in audit output across LLMs underscores the continued importance of human judgment. We discuss implications for best practices in AI-assisted PAP auditing.

### Fool’s gold: How geopolitical risk is taking some of the shine off the US dollar
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-09 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/fools-gold-how-geopolitical-risk-taking-some-shine-us-dollar
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

US Treasuries remain the world’s dominant reserve asset, but geopolitical risks can change how central banks assess the trade-off between liquidity and protection from foreign jurisdictions. This column argues that geopolitical risk can increase the demand for gold relative to sanctionable sovereign assets, putting upward pressure on the gold price and sovereign bond yields. It finds evidence of active portfolio adjustment consistent with this mechanism and shows that the adjustment is stronger in countries less aligned with the US. The results suggest that geopolitical considerations are becoming increasingly relevant alongside the traditional financial determinants of reserve allocation.

### Inflexibility and the stalled progress of American women in the labour market
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-09 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/inflexibility-and-stalled-progress-american-women-labour-market
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The labour force participation of women in the US peaked in 1997 and has stagnated since. This column argues that workplace inflexibility is the common thread running through this puzzle, using COVID-19 as a natural experiment. Women were hit harder than men in the initial downturn due to both over-representation in high-contact and inflexible service occupations and school closures. When remote work relaxed flexibility constraints, mothers’ employment rebounded strongly above pre-pandemic levels. But as return-to-office mandates have intensified, occupational inflexibility has re-emerged as a significant predictor of employment declines – and only for women.

### Plus ça change. AI and the future of economic research: From Galileo back to Socrates
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-08 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/plus-ca-change-ai-and-future-economic-research-galileo-back-socrates
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

For centuries, economic research has been throttled by scarcity of time, labour, and access to data and methods. This column argues that while AI helps dissolve that constraint, it can also exacerbate challenges the profession is already grappling with. And by blurring the traditional distinction between observing the world and modelling it, AI may even challenge our definition of good research.

### Tariff confusion
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-07 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/tariff-confusion
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The year 2025 was unprecedented in the frequency, complexity, and volatility of US trade policy interventions. This column uses a new database tracking US tariff announcements during the year to show that confusion about the current state of tariff policy led to a decline in US imports that was similar in size to the reduction caused by tariff increases alone, meaning that confusion doubled the negative impact of tariffs. Product customisation, relationship trust, and partner reputation can help firms maintain trade activity during information shocks.

### Immigration, innovation, and the geography of growth: Lessons from the Age of Mass Migration
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-07 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/immigration-innovation-and-geography-growth-lessons-age-mass-migration
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

US net migration turned negative in 2025 following a sharp tightening of immigration policies. This column turns to the Age of Mass Migration and the sharp policy reversal of the 1921 and 1924 Quota Acts to examine how immigration shapes long-run growth through its contribution to innovation. The authors find that without the Age of Mass Migration, US GDP per capita would have been 8.2% lower by 1940, while the policy that ended mass migration harmed the US comparatively little. They also demonstrate the importance of geography: the impact of immigration on productivity depends not only on how many arrive and how skilled they are, but also where they settle.

### Automatic stabilisers for multi-country issuance stablecoins
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-09-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/automatic-stabilisers-multi-country-issuance-stablecoins
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Multi-country issuance allows the same stablecoin to be issued by legally separate entities in different jurisdictions while circulating as a single, technologically fungible token. This structure creates a specific risk for Europe. In a run, holders can direct redemptions towards the jurisdiction offering the fastest, cheapest, or legally safest exit, and because Europe’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation provides stronger and less flexible redemption rights than the emerging US regime, dollar stablecoin runs originating elsewhere may concentrate on the EU issuer. This column proposes various steps to reform MiCAR and avoid multi-country issuance risks.

### ARENA 8.0 Impact Report
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-09-10 13:49 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/Zeigx7t9HuLNDoMQW/arena-8-0-impact-report
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

The impact report from ARENA’s previous iteration, ARENA 7.0, is available here . Summary: ARENA 8.0 took place at the London Initiative for Safe AI ( LISA ) between May 25th and June 26th, 2026. The purpose of this report is to evaluate ARENA 8.0’s impact according to ARENA’s four success criteria: Source high-quality participants; Upskill these talented participants in ML skills for AI safety work; Integrate participants with the existing AI safety community; Accelerate participants’ career transition into AI safety. Overall, we feel that ARENA 8.0 was successful in meeting these criteria. We are delighted that our 26 in-person participants rated their overall enjoyment of ARENA 8.0 at 8.8/10 on average, with 10/26 respondents giving a perfect 10/10 score. Criterion 1: Our participants were of a strong calibre, coming from diverse backgrounds and bringing a wealth of different expertise with them. Notably, twelve participants either held or were pursuing doctoral degrees, and eight arrived with prior work or research experience in AI safety.

### So where's this AI thing going?
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-09-10 11:46 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/HGhrDWJngx2LX9TkF/so-where-s-this-ai-thing-going
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

The Overton window is shifting with statements like An Alien Mind from OpenAI's chief scientist and Jacob Coxon's viral statement on x-risk on resigning from Anthropic. I think we should try to push it further. This is my public-facing explanation of AI progress and x-risk, where we're at and where we're probably heading. Most LessWrong readers are up on pretty much all of this and have their own opinions; I'm offering it here in case anyone is interested in my communication strategy, giving me feedback, or sending friends this brief intro to AI x-risk and timelines, and FAQ because you like my approach here. My approach is bitter medicine with just a little sugar. I want to engage people more than I want to avoid scaring them, but I want to protect their feelings enough that they can handle thinking about this enough to believe it and engage. I follow the path of saying what I actually think, because softpedaling and being vague make you sound like a liar. But I do try to prepare the reader for the shock and explain why this all sounds so weird and hard to believe.

### Does the expanding Universe stretch space or create new space?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-10 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/universe-expand-stretching-creating-space/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

It’s been nearly a full century, almost 100 years, since humanity first reached a revolutionary conclusion about the nature of our Universe: space itself cannot and does not remain static, but rather evolves with the passage of time. One of the most counterintuitive predictions of Einstein’s general relativity is that any Universe — so long as it’s uniformly (or almost uniformly) filled with one or more species of matter, radiation, or energy — cannot remain both static and stable over time. Instead, it must either expand or contract, something initially derived independently by three separate people: Alexander Friedmann (1922), who did it first on purely theoretical grounds, Georges Lemaître (1927), who was the first to conclude that the Universe was expanding by bringing theory and observation together, and Howard Robertson (1929), who independently reached the same conclusions as Lemaître for a special case of spacetimes, where Robertson’s solution was later generalized by Arthur Walker (1936).

### Astra can do a concerning amount with no chain of thought
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-09-10 02:31 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/eRmzz8J8Qkzqvzrgg/astra-can-do-a-concerning-amount-with-no-chain-of-thought
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

TLDR : Astra has 8.6x better odds of doing a reasoning task without CoT than the next best model (Fable 5.1), and can do 7.2 serial arithmetic steps in a forward pass vs 4.1 for the next best model (Gemini 3.8 Flash/Fable 5.1) Epistemic status: Heavily LLM-dependent research, and the precise results are somewhat sensitive to researcher decisions, but I’ve done enough sanity checks that I’d be surprised if the core claims were misleading One of the most striking things in the Astra report was the massive jump UK AISI found in no-CoT reasoning abilities. I was somewhat suspicious, given the size of the jump, and the many ways this kind of measurement can be misleading. Conveniently, I’ve independently been making my own no CoT reasoning benchmark [1] and tried it on there! Unfortunately, it replicates. Astra is a massive jump, and disproportionately for no CoT reasoning: No CoT Reasoning Index (NCRI) vs Epoch Capability Index (ECI) - NCRI represents ability without verbal reasoning, ECI represents overall model capability[2].

### The secret your brain uses to decide how much pain you feel
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-09 16:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/series/curiosity-lab/curiosity-lab-1/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

We treat pain like a plumbing problem. Something in the body is broken, so we block the signal with medication or cut it out with surgery. The opioid epidemic is what happens when that is the only model we have. Pain psychologist Rachel Zoffness explains why it is also an incomplete account of how pain works. Pain is constructed by the brain, out of biology, emotion, and environment at once. Most chronic back pain has no identifiable structural cause, and 90% of us are walking around with abnormal spines and no pain at all. Sleep, stress, fear, and isolation are not side factors. They are ingredients in the pain itself. This video The secret your brain uses to decide how much pain you feel is featured on Big Think .

### Leadership isn’t natural — but making intelligent decisions can be learned
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-09 15:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/business/leadership-intelligence/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Every day as a leader brings an endless flow of judgment calls, big and small. Even as you read this, you might be weighing where to focus your team’s efforts this quarter, who to hire for a role, or how to handle a slightly cheeky budget request from a colleague. You might be trying to decide whether to skip a meeting this afternoon, or how to tamp down a weird little disagreement so that it doesn’t turn into a bigger one. These choices are rarely like math problems with clean answers; you have to figure out the right approach when there isn’t really a single “right approach.” So a distinctive strength of a good leader is the ability to make choices wisely, day after day, in ways that maximize your team’s chances of success. Where does that wisdom come from? Years of experience can certainly help guide you, if you’re lucky enough to have it. But whenever you’re faced with something new, familiarity may not be enough to make you wise. That’s why — whatever stage you’re at in your career — it’s worth distinguishing between experiential wisdom and what I call deliberate wisdom .

### Can we be certain that time really exists?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-09 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/does-time-exist-182965/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

From both a philosophical and a scientific perspective, we’re taught to doubt, question, and be skeptical of everything. Even the reality of ourselves and our own experiences cannot be taken for granted, as we must make certain assumptions about how trustworthy our measurement sensors — and our own senses, for that matter — actually are in order to arrive at any satisfactory conclusions. Sure, certain things might appear real, but it’s also possible that those “real” appearances are deceiving us, and that quantities or concepts that we take for granted might be nothing more than very convincing illusions. When we consider our physical reality, however, these sorts of questions take on a different meaning. We’ve learned lots of surprising and counterintuitive lessons from our investigations of time. Time is relative, not absolute. Time always marches forward, never backward, but we still lack an explanation for the perceived arrow of time.

### NASA image shows Earth’s lumpiness — exaggerated 10,000 times
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-08 18:27 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/strange-maps/nasa-geoid-potato-earth/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

No, the Earth is not flat. But it’s not exactly round either. Nor is it — let us stop you right there, smartypants — an oblate spheroid. Close enough, but not a geoid An oblate spheroid is a geometrically smooth model of our planet that, due to its rotation, bulges at the equator and flattens at the poles. While it is close enough to be used by GPS systems, for instance, it’s not quite right: It doesn’t account for the lumpiness of our actual Earth. The technical term for the model that does is a geoid. This model shows what the ocean surface would look like if the entire planet were covered by water and sculpted only by gravity, leaving the surface slightly lumpy. What does that look like? Nothing like this. This visualization by NASA exaggerates the Earth’s surface dents and bumps by a factor of 10,000, making our cosmic home look like a slightly deflated beach ball, or a somewhat lumpy potato. Don’t get us wrong: This is not a shocking new discovery. Scientists have known for a long time about our planet’s inner potato.

### This powerful belief can help you accomplish more
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-09-08 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/agency-and-self-efficacy/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

In the 1970s, academic psychology escaped from studying just behavior to start studying mental life. Albert Bandura, the father of this cognitive revolution within social psychology, led the way in 1977 with “self-efficacy.” An “efficacy expectation” is the belief — the conscious thought — that you, yourself, can bring about the goal you desire, and this is distinguished from an “outcome expectation,” which is the belief that your goal will happen, even if you are not the agent who brings it about. Self-efficacy implies the existence of much more than the bare fact that depriving people of control results in passivity or the bare fact that control is rewarding. Those effects may or may not be caused by thoughts. Self-efficacy claims that there are conscious expectations of the future that cause trying in the future. Indeed, this turns out to be true and is the strongest sense of efficacy. Richard Lazarus, across the bay from Bandura (at Stanford) in Berkeley, proposed appraisal theory.

Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-09-10 08:00 UTC

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Notebook, in partnership with the Locarno Critics Academy, presents a series of critical correspondences in which this year’s participants look back at the Swiss film festival. In this second installment, four critics muse on cinema’s ability to splice disparate fragments together. They touch on ecstatic New York sirens, self-preservation in Iran and Palestine, performances that bear the weight of passing time, and an actress’s descent into two-faced delirium in Rome. We hear in Sans soleil (1983) that “Nothing sorts out memories from ordinary moments. It is only later that they claim remembrance, when they show their scars.” This is how I feel about memories all the time. What lingers in my mind most vividly are the seams between memories; in films, I like the joints that crack open to let imagination slip inside. I am writing this the evening after the festival has ended. On my walk back to my hotel, our nightly haunt, Pardo Bar, remains lit, yet the crowd has vanished.

Notebook, in partnership with the Locarno Critics Academy, presents a series of critical correspondences in which this year's participants look back at the Swiss film festival. In this first installment, letters exchanged between three critics contour what was on screen—broken relations, recalcitrant presences—with their experiences of Locarno beyond the frame. Photo by Alyssandra Maxine. Dear Vedant and Max, I’m writing you from the real world, which is to say, from my desk in my apartment in Brooklyn. The first few days of the festival seem very far away now, as they did even when I last saw you only five days ago. During a festival, time tends to go by like that: very quickly and also very slowly, so that ten days actually seem like two months that went by in the span of half an hour. To think back to the beginning of our time in Locarno is not unlike remembering a movie, all those images frozen in time. For a couple of weeks, reality suspended and gave way to a fantasy world contained within three or four hilly streets in a valley.

Photo by Emma Fuchs. At sunset we move like a herd. Arms laden with blankets, we snake down the gravel path, as signs in blue spray paint warn “ΠΡΟΣΟΧΗ! Use Caution!” Halfway there, the trees that bracket us part like curtains. Below, stark against the green-gold valley, we can make out a small white square—the projection screen. At a fork in the path, there is a small sign with a blue arrow on it, and the word “Temenos.” From here, the path narrows until suddenly it opens onto a clearing. In a small field, like a basin, rows of red beanbag chairs dot the ground in front of the screen, and behind them, a crescent of pink cushioned benches and a projector wait. The restoration of Eniaios (1948–1990),the magnum opus of Greek-American experimental filmmaker Gregory J. Markopoulos, has been a gradually revealed 25-year process. The experimental film comprises 168 reels organized into 22 cycles—each composed of meticulously spliced black film leader, clear leader, and brief bursting footage from Markopoulos’s early films—and is 80 hours long.

### The Armchair Answer to the Troubles
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-09-03 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/the-armchair-answer-to-the-troubles
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Illustration by Danny Ische. The 1970s was a difficult decade for cinemas across Britain and Ireland as attendances continued their long postwar decline. Most of the glamorous art deco picture palaces built during the 1930s had either closed or fallen into disrepair and comparatively few new cinemas were opened after World War II until the arrival of the multiplexes in the 1980s. While these challenges were facing exhibitors in many other countries around the world, Northern Ireland’s experience of the downturn was unique. The outbreak of violent sectarian conflict in the late 1960s, euphemistically known as the Troubles, created an environment that actively discouraged cinema attendance. Queues still formed for blockbusters such as The Godfather (1972), The Exorcist (1973), and Jaws (1975), but ongoing violence and nightly curfews made it more difficult to justify a cinema trip for more run-of-the-mill fare, with many people retreating into private spaces.

### Inherit the Wind: Kiyoshi Kurosawa’s “The Samurai and the Prisoner”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-08-31 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/inherit-the-wind-kiyoshi-kurosawa-s-the-samurai-and-the-prisoner
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The Samurai and the Prisoner (Kiyoshi Kurosawa, 2026). Ever since he first broke into the Western cinephile consciousness with Cure (1997) and Pulse (2001), Kurosawa Kiyoshi has frequently been canonized as a horror auteur. This lens has been applied and reapplied to each of his films by distributors and critics alike, with the generic conventions of horror centralized as the key means of packaging and understanding a prolific body of work. Too often, though, this understandable but shallow view of Kurosawa’s filmography has missed the forest for the Charisma tree; the release of the director’s elegant new jidaigeki , The Samurai and the Prisoner (which boasts the scarifying title of “Black Prison Castle” in Japan), affords an opportunity to revisit and reframe a remarkable career. Kurosawa has regularly stated that he has never considered himself to be a “horror director,” and that he is entirely forward looking (whether the futures he perceives are bright is another matter).

### Against The Grain: Listening after Palantir
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-09-08 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/collateral-damage/against-the-grain-listening-after-palantir
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Machine listening is transforming environmental sound into data points that support surveillance infrastructure, changing what it means to hear, argues Stephen Roddy in The Wire 512 On 28 February 2026, Shajareh Tayyebeh elementary school in Minab, Iran was hit by a missile killing over 150 people, most of them children. Initial investigations have suggested that the US was responsible for the attack. While US data mining giant Palantir has not been conclusively linked to the atrocity, the wider US-led bombing campaign has made extensive use of the Palantir-supplied Maven Smart System to coordinate similar attacks. Maven can combine large volumes of data from radar, satellite, communications and even audio sources before applying machine learning techniques to identify possible military targets. We now live in a world where our homes, schools and workplaces can become clusters of data points.

### Invisible Jukebox mix: Pamela Z
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-09-02 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/audio/tracks/invisible-jukebox-mix-pamelaz
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Listen to the music we played to Pamela Z during her Invisible Jukebox interview in The Wire 512 Each month in the magazine we play an artist or group a series of tracks which they are asked to comment on – with no prior knowledge of what they are about to hear. In The Wire 512 it is the turn of composer and vocalist Pamela Z. Here you can listen to a mix of the tracks our correspondent Marc Weidenbaum played to Z during the interview, which is published in full in The Wire 512 . To find out what she said about them, subscribers can read the interview in our online magazine library here . Or you can buy a copy of the magazine in our online shop . But first, a brief biography of our subject: San Francisco is the sort of melting pot where people who move to the city can be so essential to the cultural scene that they become not merely locals but more akin to natives after the fact. Born in Upstate New York, Pamela Z, who turned 70 this year, was raised in the suburbs of Denver and educated in music at the University of Colorado in Boulder.

### “Intimately rustic”: Motvind festival reviewed
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-08-28 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/about/contributors/peter-margasak/intimately-rustic-motvind-festival-reviewed
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

What began as an arts funding protest has grown into a constellation of site-specific performances at this rural Norwegian event, writes Peter Margasak in The Wire 511 Motvind Various venues, Rollag, Norway Thanks to limited resources and modest aims, small summer festivals all across Europe adapt a simple formula: programme a couple of touring acts and round things out with local musicians. But in Norway some of the locals are as compelling and strong as the imported headliners. As terrific as sets by Horse Lords, Bitchin Bajas and Separatist Party may be, the rest of the stylistically broad line-up at Motvind festival never feels secondary. Held in a remote valley a few hours north west of Oslo, the intimately rustic event has morphed from a political protest against the sponsorship of the nearby Kongsberg Jazz Festival by a weapons manufacturer back in 2016 into a celebration of hand-crafted music instilled with a strong improvisational aesthetic, regardless of genre.

### Wire mix: Lucy Liyou
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-08-21 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/audio/tracks/wire-mix-lucy-liyou
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

To complement her interview in The Wire 511, Korean American composer Lucy Liyou creates an exclusive Wire mix Lucy Liyou grew up in Seattle, and as a child was a talented classical pianist. Her strengths, at first, were late Romantic and 20th century music; then she discovered the likes of Andrew Hill and Cecil Taylor, which inform the fiendishly difficult piano figures in her latest album Mr Cobra. Mr Cobra allowed Liyou to explore these influences, alongside her many others, including updated Korean folk opera, horror, vocality, narrative, maximalism, and families. This mix of Liyou's influences and inspirations was compiled exclusively for The Wire .

### Digital Worlds and the Limits of Frictionless Space: Rethinking Scale and Materiality
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-09-10 07:30 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1184807/digital-worlds-and-the-limits-of-frictionless-space-rethinking-scale-and-materiality
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Historically, the way we design has been closely linked to the way we build. Architecture moved through physical testing , trial and error, projection drawings, mock-ups, and physical models : each introducing a new layer of abstraction, but still remaining tied to material, scale, and construction . The introduction of CAD significantly changed this dynamic. At first, it was largely understood as the computerization of precise geometry for construction. Over time, however, digital tools moved earlier into the design process, replacing sketching, physical trial and error, model-making, and other forms of thinking that had once kept scale and material close at hand.

### A Proposal for a New World Map and Two New Sports Complexes: This Week’s Review
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-09-10 05:30 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1184844/a-proposal-for-a-new-world-map-and-two-new-sports-complexes-this-weeks-review
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

This week's stories share a common thread: the value of looking beyond established frameworks. Two French-Moroccan architects reflected on what architecture can learn from Moroccan culture and local knowledge, while the United Nations General Assembly voted to promote world map projections that more accurately represent the relative size of continents- a symbolic shift that challenges centuries of Eurocentric geographical representation, with direct implications for how we understand Africa and the Global South. A similar spirit of questioning convention runs through this week's featured projects, from city governments repurposing abandoned buildings for circular construction to the German Pavilion for the upcoming Venice Architecture Biennale addressing social isolation and democratic coexistence.

### Oslo Architecture City Guide: 12 Buildings Contrasting Historic and Contemporary Design
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1184689/oslo-architecture-city-guide-12-buildings-contrasting-historic-and-contemporary-design
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Oslo is the capital of Norway as well as its largest city, serving as the country's political, economic, and cultural center. Nestled at the head of the Oslofjord and surrounded by forested hills, it's home to roughly 700,000 people in the city , with over a million living in the greater metropolitan area . Founded around a thousand years ago, Oslo has a long history as a trading post and administrative seat. However, much of its modern character was shaped after a major fire in the seventeenth century led to a full rebuilding under Danish rule, when it was briefly renamed Christiania. Today it consistently ranks among the world's most livable cities , prized for its blend of urban life and easy access to nature.

### Bargain with demons and boat with the dead in joyful RPG heist quest anthology Thousand Hells
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/bargain-with-demons-and-boat-with-the-dead-in-joyful-rpg-heist-quest-anthology-thousand-hells
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

You'd expect a game called Thousand Hells: The Underworld Heists to be on the depressing side, but this choose-your-own-adventure RPG anthology from the designers of King of Dragon Pass is an absolute tonic. It's a witty procession of Orphic descents, in which you assemble a crew of four garrulous weirdos and pitch them against quest scenarios that might involve talking trees, holy riddlers, and conduits of boiling blood. There's an infectious, venturesome lightness to the game, which somehow persists alongside dire blunders and supernatural calamities that may see members of your party being cursed, punctured or unhinged. I've only completed a couple of the 10 heists across two hours of play, which makes me reluctant to call this an official review, but it's got my provisional recommendation. Come, reader - get thee to Pandemonium apace. Read more

Physint , the action espionage game Hideo Kojima and Kojima Productions announced a couple of years ago , will no longer be published by initial backers PlayStation. Instead, it'll be published by Xbox, who're already handing those duties for upcoming Kojima horror flick OD . Sony have tried to paint this switch as an amicable parting of ways with the long-time Metal Gear Solid director's studio, but Kojima himself has claimed that PlayStation initially informed him they planned to cancel Physint, triggering a three month long hunt for a new publisher to keep the game alive. Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02575-5
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 03 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02575-5 In this Perspective, Haubrock et al. argue that today’s scientific structures constrain creativity and that strategic conformity is a rational response. They discuss how this holds back early-career researchers from reaching their full potential as scientists.

### The gender gap reconsidered
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02448-x
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 03 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02448-x Gender polarization in fields such as engineering or nursing has served as a barrier to students whose genders do not conform to these norms. ### A fake spider at the tip of this snake's tail helps it lure birds: Scientists get their first look inside it
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-fake-spider-snake-tail-lure.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Lots of animals use mimicry to survive. Some copy the appearance of threatening predators, and others have evolved to resemble plants or rocks so they can blend into the background and avoid being attacked. But a viper from Iran has found an even stranger strategy. The tip of this snake's tail looks like a spider, complete with a bulbous body and long dangling legs. And when a bird swoops in to try to eat this "spider," the snake springs into action and eats the bird.

### The E3 ubiquitin ligase LOP1 targets PIF4 to control plant morphogenesis
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)00892-4?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Plant architecture is regulated by multiple cues. Song et al. reveal a phyB-LOP1-PIF4 module coordinating morphogenic development. phyB and LOP1 mutually stabilize each other, while LOP1 mediates PIF4 ubiquitination and degradation. Conversely, PIF4 directly binds the LOP1 promoter to activate its transcription.

### Microbial GAIN domains undergo autoproteolysis and enable release of diverse cell-surface-associated proteins
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)00951-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Brogan and Rudner report the identification of a microbial autoproteolytic domain that is structurally homologous to the GAIN domain of eukaryotic adhesion GPCRs. Rather than participating in transmembrane signaling, these domains tether diverse domains to the cell surface and are proposed to release them in response to mechanical stimuli.

### Ancestral karyotype reconstruction provides insights into stepwise chromosome evolution and bract coloration in the banana family
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01002-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Fu et al. reconstruct the ancestral karyotype of the banana family and reveal stepwise chromosome evolution through lineage-specific rearrangements. They show that breakpoint-associated regions are enriched with anthocyanin-related genes and transcription factors, providing insights into the potential role of genome rearrangements in bract coloration diversity.

### The proteasome promotes cytokinetic abscission by relieving an Aurora B-dependent inhibition of ESCRT-III dynamics
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01004-3?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Dibsy et al. show that proteasomal degradation of the Aurora B kinase is required for cytokinetic abscission in human cells. Aurora B degradation reduces the phosphorylation of its substrate, CHMP4C, at the intercellular bridge, which in turn promotes the ESCRT-III polymer dynamics required for cytokinetic abscission.

### Coherent oscillations across the human brain, spinal cord, and muscle network
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01001-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Spedden et al. use optically pumped magnetometers to record magnetic fields from the human brain and spinal cord at the same time. Coherent 10–35 Hz activity links the sensorimotor cortex, cervical spinal cord, and hand muscle. The coupling is bidirectional, and the cord does not act as a passive relay.

### Uptake via the leaf surface allows plants to perceive volatiles with closed stomata
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01015-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

If and how plants detect stress volatiles when their stomata are closed is unclear. Yu et al. show that the succulent CAM plant Kalanchoë laxiflora can take up herbivore-induced volatiles via the leaf surface, which enables robust volatile perception independently of stomatal opening.

### [Series] Adverse pregnancy outcomes and long-term cardiovascular disease risk
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01235-3/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pregnancy provides a unique physiological stress test for the cardiovascular system, during which, adverse pregnancy outcomes (APOs) can unmask latent susceptibility to future disease. Common complications, including hypertensive disorders of pregnancy (HDP), gestational diabetes, and preterm birth (delivery before 37 weeks’ gestation), identify women at substantially higher long-term risk of cardiovascular morbidity and mortality compared with women without a history of APOs. These excess risks likely reflect the combined effects of pre-existing cardiometabolic and genetic susceptibility, as well as the haemodynamic and metabolic stressors of pregnancy, heralding accelerated risk factor trajectories, relative impairment in endothelial and microvascular function, and early disease onset.

### [Series] Cardiovascular disease in pregnancy: physiology, global burden, risk stratification, and opportunities in care
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-09-10 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01234-1/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Cardiovascular disease remains one of the leading causes of maternal mortality worldwide and a major driver of long-term morbidity. Yet, pregnancy-related cardiovascular complications are often under-recognised and variably managed across global settings. This three-part Series examines the continuum of cardiovascular risk surrounding pregnancy—from preconception through to lifelong surveillance. In the first paper in this Series, we describe the haemodynamic changes of pregnancy and global trends of congenital and acquired heart disease before pregnancy, evaluate established risk-stratification tools, and discuss the importance of multidisciplinary care.

### Towards a Quantum Erlangen Program
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09167
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09167v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: The classical Erlangen Program sought to classify metric spaces entirely in terms of their symmetries. In physical spacetimes, these symmetries define transformations between classical reference frames, yielding a one-to-one correspondence between frame transformations and the underlying geometry. More recently, the classical notion of ideal frame has been extended to the quantum regime, by considering observers as embodied physical systems, subject to the laws of quantum mechanics. Here, we build on this approach, but outline an alternative definition of the term `quantum reference frame', which differs somewhat from the mainstream view. We then show how the new definition can be used to construct a simple model of Planck-scale spacetime, which makes contact with existing quantum gravity phenomenology.

### Groups of Worldview Transformations Implied by Einstein's Special Principle of Relativity over Arbitrary Ordered Fields
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09168
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09168v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: In 1978, Yu. F. Borisov presented an axiom system using a few basic assumptions and four explicit axioms, the fourth being a formulation of the relativity principle; and he demonstrated that this axiom system had (up to choice of units) only two models: a relativistic one in which worldview transformations are Poincar\'e transformations and a classical one in which they are Galilean. In this paper, we reformulate Borisov's original four axioms within an intuitively simple, but strictly formal, first-order logic framework, and convert his basic background assumptions into explicit axioms. Instead of assuming that the structure of physical quantities is the field of real numbers, we assume only that they form an ordered field. This allows us to investigate how Borisov's theorem depends on the structure of quantities. We demonstrate (as our main contribution) how to construct Euclidean, Galilean, and Poincar\'e models of Borisov's axiom system over every non-Archimedean field.

### $f(T,\mathcal{L}_m)$ Cosmology Embedded in a Viscous Barotropic Fluid
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09169
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09169v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We examine the cosmological dynamics of a viscous fluid within the framework of $f(T,\mathcal{L}_m)$ gravity by using a barotropic equation of state for cosmic fluid. The modified Friedmann equations are used to construct an analytical Hubble model. Then $H(z)$, Pantheon+SH0ES, DESI DR II BAO, and Cosmic Microwave Background datasets are used in a Bayesian Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis to constrain its free parameters. Physically viable estimations of the Hubble constant and barotropic equation of state parameter are given by the obtained best-fit values. Based on the constrained parameters, we examine the redshift dependence of deceleration parameter, effective equation of state, dark energy equation of state, the effective pressure and viscous pressure quantities. While the deceleration parameter reveals how the universe evolved from an initially decelerating state to its present accelerating expansion, both equation of state parameters persist in the quintessence regime in the late epoch.

### Noether's theorems for first and second order field theories in flat spacetimes
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09170
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09170v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Technical derivations of the Noether identity (that is, showing and explaining all steps using methods of the calculus of variations), as well as of Noether's first and second theorems, are typically presented in explicit detail in the mathematics literature for scalar functions with first order derivatives in the action. This presentation has drawbacks for physicists wishing to learn these techniques to apply to physical field theories that have higher rank tensor fields (potentials) or higher orders of derivatives in the action. Motivated by this, and heavily influenced by the calculus of variations presentation of Gelfand and Fomin, we detail complete derivations of the Noether identity and Noether's theorems for first and second order field theories with tensor fields of arbitrary rank in flat (e.g., Minkowski) spacetimes, showing all steps explicitly.

### Testing the Anton-Schmidt dark energy model with the DESI DR2 measurements
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09171
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09171v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We present a comparative cosmological analysis of the Anton-Schmidt, $\Lambda$CDM, and CPL models using baryon acoustic oscillation measurements from the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument Data Release 2, combined with the Planck PR4 (NPIPE) CamSpec Cosmic Microwave Background likelihoods and three Type Ia supernova catalogues: Pantheon$+$, DES-Dovekie, and Union3. We use Markov Chain Monte Carlo analyses to constrain the parameters of the Anton-Schmidt model against each dataset combination. We find that the Anton-Schmidt model provides a good fit to current cosmological observations. The Anton-Schmidt model provides a good fit to the current cosmological observations but does not provide a significant alleviation of either the $H_0$ or the $S_8$ tensions. The Anton-Schmidt parameter is tightly constrained to $B \simeq -0.38$, indicating that the logarithmic correction becomes relevant only at late times.

### Strong Gravitational Lensing by Lorentzian-Euclidean Black Hole
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-09-10 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09172
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2609.09172v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We investigate the strong gravitational lensing properties of the Lorentzian Euclidean black hole, a spacetime in which the horizon at $r=2M$ is not a coordinate singularity but a genuine surface of signature change, with the associated curvature singularities removed by two regularization parameters, $\rho$ and $k$. Starting from the null geodesic equations, we derive the photon sphere, the critical impact parameter, and the strong deflection limit coefficients, and use them to obtain the deflection angle and the full set of strong lensing observables, namely the angular position of the relativistic images, their angular separation, the relative magnification, and the differential time delay between successive images. We show that the photon sphere and critical impact parameter increase with $\rho$ and decrease with $k$, indicating that the two parameters have opposite effects on the optical geometry, and evaluate the resulting observables numerically for the supermassive black holes Sgr A* and M87*.

### MTM's selfie footage of BepiColombo's eight-year journey
Source: ESA News
Published: 2026-09-09 12:00 UTC
URL: https://www.esa.int/ESA_Multimedia/Videos/2026/09/MTM_s_selfie_footage_of_BepiColombo_s_eight-year_journey
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Video: 00:04:20 Witness how over the last eight years, the ESA/JAXA BepiColombo mission has been travelling through the inner Solar System and aligning itself with Mercury's orbit. So far, it has travelled 9.9 billion km and completed nine 'planetary flybys' to help steer its way. In the video, you can see the spacecraft fly past Earth once, Venus twice, and Mercury six times. Since launching on 20 October 2018, BepiColombo has been travelling as a composite spacecraft made up of ESA's Mercury Planetary Orbiter (MPO), JAXA's Mercury Magnetospheric Orbiter (Mio), and the Mercury Transfer Module (MTM) that carried the two orbiters to Mercury. We've shared photos and videos before, taken by the spacecraft's three monitoring cameras ( M-CAMs ) on MTM. But this is the first time we've compiled them into a video of the mission's complete journey so far. The monitoring cameras were designed to keep an eye on the spacecraft itself.

Source: Entomology Today
Published: 2026-08-11 17:30 UTC

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

This post Bad Blood: Drug-Spiked Bloodmeal Effective for Eliminating Female Mosquitoes appeared first on Entomology Today - Brought to you by the Entomological Society of America . To use the sterile insect technique against mosquitoes, females must be removed from the lab-raised populations before release. A new study shows that female mosquitoes' taste for blood can be used against them by lacing a bloodmeal with insecticide, significantly accelerating the sex-separation process before sterile males can be released. [Read more] The post Bad Blood: Drug-Spiked Bloodmeal Effective for Eliminating Female Mosquitoes appeared first on Entomology Today .

### New evidence suggests vast hidden magma systems inside Mars
Source: Geology Page
Published: 2026-09-08 08:57 UTC
URL: https://www.geologypage.com/2026/09/new-evidence-suggests-vast-hidden-magma-systems-inside-mars.html
